But then if someone told me to slow down or keep normal shop hours, I’d get irritated fast and snap at them.\n\nDoctor: During those wired periods, did you find yourself talking much more than usual?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t know that I noticed myself talking more than usual. Maybe I was a little more chatty, but nothing that really stands out to me.\n\nDoctor: During those wired periods, did you do things that were unusually risky or impulsive for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I can’t think of anything that was, like, dangerous or way out of character. Maybe I made decisions faster than usual, but nothing specific is coming to mind.\n\nDoctor: During one of those wired periods, did it ever cause major problems in your life, like getting you into serious trouble at work or leading to hospitalization?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it caused real problems. I missed a big custom delivery for a client, and my shop records got so messed up I’ve had to stop taking new work for now. My family’s been checking on me more too, because they could tell I’d gotten pretty far away from my normal routines.\n\nDoctor: During those wired periods, did you feel more powerful, talented, or important than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I did. After I won that ribbon, I felt like I’d proved I was on another level. Like I could take on every custom order and make each one into some kind of showpiece that nobody else around here could really match.\n\nDoctor: During one of those wired periods, did you ever believe something that other people said wasn’t true or realistic?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I felt really confident, maybe too confident, but I don’t remember believing something that people were telling me was flat-out not real or impossible.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I mean, I felt really confident, maybe too confident, but I don’t remember believing something that people were telling me was flat-out not real or impossible.\n\nDoctor: Did one of those wired, high-energy periods last at least 7 days?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I don’t think there was anything specific like that I can point to when it started.\n\nDoctor: During that two-week high-energy period, were you much more driven than usual to start projects or get things done?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I was trying to get everything moving at once. I started lining up new commissions, messaging old customers, making plans for a website, and sketching out this whole Appalachian collection. These past couple weeks have been like that. I don’t remember it being a big issue when I was a kid, at least not in a way that stood out. I got through school okay, but I don’t know if I was just used to it or if it’s more of a recent thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt unusually energized or wired for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: Yeah, there have been times like that. I’d be at the bench and just couldn’t sit still. I’d start sketching a new watch case, then jump to emailing suppliers, then start rearranging the display trays, even after we were supposed to be closed. It was like I had to keep doing something.\n\nDoctor: During those wired periods, did you need much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah. During those times I’d sleep maybe two or three hours and still wake up feeling clear-headed, like I was ready to go. I’d get up before sunrise and want to start cutting stones or posting new designs.\n\nDoctor: During those wired periods, did you feel unusually euphoric or on top of the world?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I did. After the show, I remember feeling really lit up, like I was bigger than myself or something—like everything I was doing was important. But then if someone told me to slow down or keep normal shop hours, I’d get irritated fast and snap at them.\n\nDoctor: During those wired periods, did you find yourself talking much more than usual?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t know that I noticed myself talking more than usual. Maybe I was a little more chatty, but nothing that really stands out to me.\n\nDoctor: During those wired periods, did you do things that were unusually risky or impulsive for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I can’t think of anything that was, like, dangerous or way out of character. Maybe I made decisions faster than usual, but nothing specific is coming to mind.\n\nDoctor: During one of those wired periods, did it ever cause major problems in your life, like getting you into serious trouble at work or leading to hospitalization?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it caused real problems. I missed a big custom delivery for a client, and my shop records got so messed up I’ve had to stop taking new work for now. My family’s been checking on me more too, because they could tell I’d gotten pretty far away from my normal routines.\n\nDoctor: During those wired periods, did you feel more powerful, talented, or important than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I did. After I won that ribbon, I felt like I’d proved I was on another level. Like I could take on every custom order and make each one into some kind of showpiece that nobody else around here could really match.\n\nDoctor: During one of those wired periods, did you ever believe something that other people said wasn’t true or realistic?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I felt really confident, maybe too confident, but I don’t remember believing something that people were telling me was flat-out not real or impossible.\n\nDoctor: Did one of those wired, high-energy periods last at least 7 days?\n\nPatient: Yeah. The worst one started right after I got that award at the jewelry show, and it kept going for about two weeks.\n\nDoctor: When that two-week high-energy period started, had you been using alcohol or any drugs?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. I don’t think there was anything specific like that I can point to when it started.\n\nDoctor: During that two-week high-energy period, were you much more driven than usual to start projects or get things done?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I was trying to get everything moving at once. I started lining up new commissions, messaging old customers, making plans for a website, and sketching out this whole Appalachian collection. It felt like I had to do all of it right then.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah. These past couple weeks have been like that. Most days I wake up with this heavy, hollow feeling, like everything’s lost its shine, and I’ve cried in my truck before opening the shop.\n\nDoctor: During these past couple of weeks, have you had thoughts of wanting to die or of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I don’t have a plan to hurt myself, but I keep thinking my family and my clients would be better off if I wasn’t around. It scares me how often that thought comes up.\n\nDoctor: Have you used cocaine, methamphetamine, or other stimulants around the times when you felt unusually energized and needed very little sleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I don’t think so, not around those times. I haven’t noticed a connection like that.\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy periods, were you drinking more alcohol than usual?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I do drink sometimes, but I haven’t really paid attention to whether it was more during those high-energy stretches. I can’t say I noticed a clear pattern.\n\nDoctor: Did your current depression begin after that two-week high-energy period ended?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. The depressed, guilty feeling has been hanging over me for about a month now.\n\nDoctor: During these past couple of weeks, have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I’ve still done some of the usual stuff here and there, but I haven’t really paid attention to whether I’m enjoying it less. It’s hard to say.\n\nDoctor: During that two-week high-energy period, were you hospitalized for your mental health?\n\nPatient: No, I wasn’t hospitalized during that time."
